                                              215
Appendix B

Reproducibility and Provenance

B.1     What this appendix establishes

This appendix records three things that should not be confused with one another. The
first is what the source repository prescribes for a rerun: a pinned container, a pinned
dependency set, a headline training command, and acceptance tolerances. These instruc-
tions do not guarantee that this public document package contains every dependency,
checkpoint or source payload. The second is what the repository verifies about itself
without re-running any model: an audit suite driven by a single entry point, and a de-
terministic ledger check that recomputes the reported arithmetic directly from surviving
evidence files. The third is provenance: which parts of this work were produced by the
Semester 3 group, which by the individual contribution reported here, and what was lost
along the way.



B.2     The pinned runtime

The container definition is Dockerfile, built from nvidia/cuda:12.4.1-cudnn-de
vel-ubuntu22.04. It installs Python 3.12 through the deadsnakes archive rather than
the distribution default, with the in-file comment that Python 3.12 “matches the frozen
runtime validated 106/106”. It sets PYTHONHASHSEED=42 and a fixed LANG/LC_ALL of
C.UTF-8; stamps the build commit into an OCI org.opencontainers.image.revi
sion label and a .build_info file; fixes SEED=42, WANDB_MODE=offline, HF_HUB_
DISABLE_TELEMETRY=1 and OMP_NUM_THREADS=8; self-checks by importing the core
stack at build time; and runs under tini.
The authoritative pin set is requirements.txt, headed as “pinned to the frozen E1
runtime lock … validated 106/106 on this stack”. Its exact pins include torch==2.7.1,


                                          216
numpy==2.2.6,      transformers==5.5.4,     trl==1.2.0,     datasets==4.8.4,
accelerate==1.13.0,       peft==0.19.1,     huggingface-hub==1.11.0        and
wandb==0.21.0, with bounded ranges for scipy, safetensors, tokenizers, rliable,
math-verify, latex2sympy2-extended and pytest, alongside the framework clients
tinker, tinker-cookbook and atroposlib.
The 106/106 figure is the recorded outcome of the frozen-runtime validation, which
execution-notes.md states as passing the complete pinned pilot/preregistration suite
on Python 3.12.13 with the pinned versions listed above, and the focused amendment,
protocol, launcher and verifier suite passing 55/55. The same record binds five artefacts
by SHA-256 — protocol document, screening manifest, launch manifest, launch finger-
print and source binding — and states that a mismatch in package, Python, A100, FLOP,
token or gradient conditions fails closed rather than proceeding.



B.3      The reviewer’s entry point

REPRODUCE.md is the reviewer-facing document. Its fastest path is the structural smoke
test at platform_modal/scripts/smoke_test.sh, which runs in about two minutes
without a Tinker key and about eight when a live key is supplied so that the wire protocol
is exercised; the keyless path needs no GPU and no account, and is offline only once the
GSM8K dataset is cached, because one step loads it.
The headline result is GRPO on Qwen3-8B over GSM8K for 30 steps at LoRA rank
32, group size 8 and seed 42, recorded as a peak per-step training accuracy (the bi-
nary reward) of 62.5 % with a last-10 average of 34.4 %; these summarise the training
curve of the seed-42 run, not a held-out evaluation, and Table 1.1 lists them beside
the other Qwen3-8B GSM8K figures they are sometimes confused with. Reviewer-side
reruns are accepted within ±5 percentage points on the last-10 figure and ±10 points
on peak, justified in the same document by the residual non-determinism discussed
in §B.10. Per-seed reference values and W&B run identifiers are tabulated for seeds
42, 123, 456 and 789, with seed 1024 marked as a reviewer-only rerun; the canon-
ical multi-seed set is {42, 123, 456, 789, 1024, 2048, 4096, 8192, 16384,
32768}. The group-size ablation is expected to reproduce a peak of 50.0 % at G=2,
75.0 % at G=4, 100 % at G=8 and 71.9 % at G=16, with last-10 values of 37.5 %, 52.1
%, 84.4 % and 38.0 %. Five-seed aggregation runs through utils/stats.py, which
now accepts --bootstrap-samples (default 10,000) and prints the mean, standard
error and a percentile-bootstrap 95% interval; its --format and --rliable flags are
accepted but not yet acted on, and it reads a results/<experiment>/seed_<N>/*.j
sonl layout that the trainer does not write, so a reviewer must write one metrics file per


                                           217
seed first, as REPRODUCE.md §3.4 states. utils/verify_results.py checks a rerun
against platform_hybrid/paper/expected_results.json at those tolerances. It
parses the per-seed avg_last10 / peak_reward block that grpo_cli actually prints,
and it exits non-zero when a value is outside tolerance or missing, when no result file
matches an expected experiment, or (exit 2) when the expectations file or results direc-
tory is missing. Before the 3 October fix it parsed a line format the trainer never prints
and exited 0 when nothing matched, so a failed rerun would have been reported as a pass;
tests/test_verify_results.py now covers both formats and the failure cases. One
limitation remains: every seed is checked against the single seed-42 headline (0.344 /
0.625), so a seed whose recorded values differ by more than the tolerance, such as seed
456 with a 100% peak, is reported as outside tolerance.
The cost is stated plainly: about five GPU-hours on a single A100-80GB for the head-
line run, against roughly 446 GPU-hours for a full paper reproduction. Dataset prove-
nance is tabulated with pinned revisions — GSM8K at 740312a with 7,473 training
and 1,319 test rows, plus MATH-500, HumanEval, ArenaHard Auto, NoRobots and
OpenThoughts3 each at a named snapshot — with exact shas in ARTIFACT.md §3.4.



B.4      Where the documentation has drifted from the code

Four discrepancies existed between the repository’s prose and its live tree;
REPRODUCE.md was corrected on 3 October 2026 and ARTIFACT.md was not. The train-
ing driver has moved to platform_tinker/grpo_gsm8k_base.py. REPRODUCE.md
now invokes it at that path, while ARTIFACT.md still invokes grpo_gsm8k_base.py
at the repository root; the flags are unchanged, because the CLI in platform_tink
er/tinkerrl/grpo_cli.py carries a compatibility entry point, legacy_main, that
translates the documented flag spellings onto the current ones. The same correction
pointed REPRODUCE.md’s Modal, figure and Atropos commands at their live paths (p
latform_modal/scripts/modal_run_experiments.py, platform_modal/scri
pts/make_paper_figures.py, platform_tinker/atropos), removed an Atropos
--seed flag the launcher does not accept, replaced the expected-output blocks with
the format the trainer prints, and added a verify step to the quick start. Every doc-
umented command file now exists and the Python entry points answer --help; the
Atropos scripts need tenacity, latex2sympy2_extended and atroposlib, which
requirements.txt installs in the Docker image but the local development environ-
ment lacks. The analysis scripts platform_modal/scripts/p5p8/p5_headline_c
is_full.py and platform_modal/scripts/length_bias_iter24.py previously
resolved their repository root and results directory incorrectly and created directories on
import; both now resolve paths from the repository root, and their regenerated outputs

                                           218
are the source of the corrected §6.5.3 and §7.1 values. ARTIFACT.md names the smoke
test at scripts/smoke_test.sh rather than at platform_modal/scripts/smoke_te
st.sh, though REPRODUCE.md gives the correct path. The pins quoted in prose are stale:
REPRODUCE.md §9 states that requirements.txt pins transformers>=4.56.2,<4
.60.0 and a datasets<3.2 bound, whereas the live file pins transformers==5.5.4
and datasets==4.8.4, and ARTIFACT.md §3.3 quotes a third, older set; similarly,
ARTIFACT.md describes the environment as Python 3.10 from the Ubuntu 22.04 archive,
whereas the Dockerfile installs Python 3.12 via deadsnakes and the frozen-runtime
record validates on Python 3.12.13. The ARTIFACT.md items are explained by that doc-
ument’s frozen status — it records a Semester 3 artefact at commit 9019b5c946f17ff
e4761b5f4eac13b86d1e51cc0, dated 19 April 2026 — but REPRODUCE.md §9 is live
guidance, not a frozen record, and carries the same defect.



B.5      The audit apparatus

The integrity suite is driven by a single entry point, platform_local/run_all_
audits.py, which imports a shared result interface from utils/audit_utils.py
and registers its audits as a tuple of name-and-function pairs. As it stands in this
tree, that tuple contains nine audits: claim_issues, sync_issues, anon_issues,
strength_issues, package_issues, workflow_issues, export_guard_issues,
caveat_issues and scientific_issues. Each is implemented by a correspond-
ing platform_local/ module carrying a __main__ block, so each can also run on
its own; tests/test_audit_runner.py asserts that the registered modules and the
platform_local/*_audit.py files are the same set.
Some repository documents give different counts, and the difference is historical. At the
blind-review sweep the suite was thirteen audits, registered as a list of script filenames at
the repository root and executed as subprocesses; the committed log of that run records
METRIC audits_total=13 with ten passing and three failing, and the same sweep
states that all seventeen *_audit.py scripts then at the root were executed, with the
suite covering thirteen of them. The registry now holds nine audits, all passing: a prun-
ing commit removed obsolete audits, and a later restructuring replaced the subprocess
runner with the in-process interface, so the seventeen-script description has narrowed by
the same route. No script now prints a metric line directly; the line is constructed in one
place, AuditResult.metric_line() in utils/audit_utils.py, and emitted by the
nine audit modules plus the runner. A repository-wide scan finds several dozen files
matching *_audit.py, but that population is not a suite: it includes unrelated analyses,
test modules and a third-party file in the virtual environment.



                                            219
The output contract is uniform. AuditResult.metric_line() emits METRIC
<name>=<issue_count>, so every audit reports one machine-readable line whose
right-hand side is the number of issues it found, and an audit passes exactly when
that count is zero. render_suite prints each audit’s metric line and issue codes, then
three suite-level lines — METRIC suite_issues, METRIC audits_total and METRIC
audits_passing — before either listing the failing audits by name or stating that all
audits are passing. The entry point exits 0 when the suite passes and 1 otherwise, which
makes it usable as a gate; the packaging step runs the suite before exporting and refuses
to package if it fails. The runner’s own test asserts this rendering against a synthetic two-
audit suite, checking for METRIC audits_total=2 and METRIC suite_issues=1.



B.6      The deterministic ledger check

Where the suite above audits the submission package’s internal consistency, a second
instrument audits the campaign’s reported figures. outputs/verification/LEDGE
R_CODE_CHECK_2026-09-19.json, under schema ledger-code-check-v1, records
eleven checks of which eleven passed and none failed. They cover E1, E8 coverage, E9,
E11 and E14 counts and hashes and the lost-source probes; they do not cover the E10
score, E8 accuracy, the E2, E4, E5 and E7 lanes or anything recorded after 19 September,
and the E14 check confirmed the official scorer’s 4,426-row denominator rather than the
4,428-row denominator this report uses. Each recomputes a claim from the underlying
evidence rather than trusting a summary of it, and the four classes of check prove four
different things.

The distinction matters under questioning. An exact division proves the arithmetic is not
a rounding convenience or a transcription error; it cannot prove the underlying evidence
is sound. A receipt-presence check proves coverage claims are backed by artefacts; it
cannot prove those artefacts were produced correctly. A hash-equality check proves


                                          221
identity between two artefacts — here, between what was sealed and what was later val-
idated — but does not prove correct execution or scientific validity. An absence probe
establishes non-discovery within its dated search scope; it cannot establish universal ab-
sence or deletion. The four checks answer different questions rather than forming a sin-
gle evidence ranking. Two instances show the classes in use. The historical E1 records
describe matching request and validation bindings without establishing that execution
occurred. Private request identifiers are withheld. The E9 records show 41 graded rows
resolving to 40 unique competitions against a denominator of 75 in the streaming arm
and exactly one valid grade in the merged-VLLM arm, which is the check that confirms
the two serving arms were never pooled.



B.7      The receipt model

A receipt in this campaign is a durable JSON artefact binding an outcome to the inputs
that produced it, and the binding carries the evidential weight rather than the file’s exis-
tence. The clearest statement of the principle is the receipt-bundle auditor’s docstring,
which records that the campaign and the receipt builders are kept deliberately separate
from the auditor, and that the auditor “never imports a provider SDK, contacts a service,
launches a job, or treats status/online/a URL/ID alone as provenance”. The auditor
reconstructs contract coverage, receipt predicates, canonical hash rules, budget caps and
cross-artefact bindings, carrying its expectations as constants rather than reading them
from what it checks — suite counts of 12 training, 14 primary-evaluation and 26 total
— so a receipt set that quietly loses a suite fails the audit rather than redefining it.
Receipts bind four dimensions of a run: protocol, runtime, source and event order. The
frozen-runtime record applies that discipline to the runtime dimension alone, binding
five artefacts — protocol document, screening manifest, launch manifest, launch fin-
gerprint and source binding — by SHA-256 and failing closed on any mismatch. The
judgment layer for subjectively scored questions follows the same pattern: one durable
receipt per judgment binds the state bytes, the question text and the raw answer, and
battery execution is gated on a health check rather than the service’s availability, so a
degraded answer cannot be laundered into a valid one. Where a lane could not be scored,
its terminal state is recorded in the ledger of record with a named reopen condition, so
the absence of a result is itself receipted.




                                            222
B.8      Evidence loss in the finish era

The historical record reports that four finish-era execution-source components are un-
available: an E1 recovery driver, restored E9 runtime dependencies, an E6 receipt val-
idator and an E1 multilingual metadata driver. This limits end-to-end reproduction.
Operational scratch-workspace names, executed request identifiers and private source
paths are omitted from this public derivative.
The four lost execution-source paths do not themselves contain the score artefacts, but
their loss limits end-to-end reproducibility. The surviving evidence supports three nar-
rower observations. First, none of the four paths is the source of any reported score.
The E1 and E9 replacement lanes were eventually run on 27 September with new thin
runners rather than the lost code (§8.4). The loss still bears on E1: the source-context
collector had to be re-implemented for that run and was checked byte-for-byte against
the original on only three recorded tasks, which is one reason the 1/190 and 4/110 fig-
ures are not pooled. Second, the sealed requests, validation receipts, regression fixtures
and the 10-of-10 targeted-test log from the lost E1 code all survive, so a faithful re-
implementation is constrained by surviving fixtures rather than free to invent behaviour;
that re-implementation has since landed under a tracked path and passes the lost suite’s
recorded regressions, four process-group and six cleanup. Third, the loss is confined to
finish-era iteration code: the replacement-lane native runners survive in tracked loca-
tions, including a 515-line multilingual runner carrying no reference to the deleted tree.
The policy adopted in response — that execution sources live in tracked repository paths
and never in untracked scratch trees — is the durable outcome of the incident.



B.9      The Semester 3 and Semester 4 provenance bound-
         ary

The boundary between inherited and individual work is a git tag.
capstone-final-2026-04-25 resolves to commit 21a99ef766543a1ca86bfb
ad188445d3596ea73c, dated 23 April 2026, whose subject line records the final
metric-separation rewrite that segregated training rewards, held-out capability and
proxy diagnostics. Two hundred and fifty commits precede that tag. A reviewer can
reproduce the state at the boundary by adding it as a worktree, git worktree add
../tinker-rl-lab-sem3 capstone-final-2026-04-25, which materialises the
Semester 3 tree without disturbing the current checkout.
The Semester 3 group comprised six students — Arvind C R, Sandhya Jeyaraj, Madhu
Kumara L, Mohammad Rafi, Dhruva N Murthy and Arumugam Chetty K — un-


                                           223
der guides Anwesh Reddy Paduri and Narayana Darapaneni. Their work is the
multi-framework benchmark infrastructure and the initial GRPO baseline experiments.
Semester 4 was carried out individually under Ramesh Prakash Guledgudd, and the
declaration facing this report assigns to that contribution the research direction, the ex-
perimental expansion, the statistical analyses, the P1–P8 paper series and the E1–E14
campaign. The solo-authorship commit 282e6b4f0f158de11b3f5bb4656e2d871f6
8f5c0, dated 7 July 2026, records that division at paper level. The provenance record
states that 584 commits and 5,050 changed files separated the boundary from the tree as
it then stood, and that the eight paper PDFs were compiled at commit 006e90a38e4d
abe2d0b4778be01dc7b27f2d3462 on 9 July 2026, one recompiled the following day
after a findings-ledger rename. Those counts carry an as-of date rather than describing
the current tree, so a reviewer recomputing them will obtain a larger number.
One consequence of this history shapes the repository’s structure: the implementation
remains shared, with experiment drivers, result tables, figures, LaTeX sources and audit
scripts referencing one another across the boundary, and the provenance record is explicit
that this is why the code is not split into separate trees. What the boundary separates
is authorship of the research contribution, not access to the machinery. Two reporting
rules follow and are enforced throughout this report: original-contract and replacement-
scope results are never pooled, with no cross-suite aggregate, which the ledger check
independently verifies through the arm-separation and component-split checks; and no
replacement benchmark’s score is presented as the score of the benchmark it replaces,
with every substitution named as such at its point of use.



B.10       Residual non-determinism and the limits of repro-
           duction

Not everything here is reproducible in the strict sense, and the tolerances exist because
of it. Three sources of residual non-determinism survive even with fixed seeds and de-
terministic cuDNN settings: remote LoRA training is not bit-deterministic across sched-
uler decisions, with runs replayed months apart drifting by two to four percentage points
on GSM8K last-10, documented through three reruns of the same seed-123 configura-
tion; some matmul operations on H100 and A100 hardware introduce non-determinism
through CUDA and cuBLAS; and sampling at temperature 0.8 with top-p 0.95 executes
in a remote virtual machine, so a fixed seed does not fix the sampled trajectory. The
record states the consequence itself: run-to-run variance on this setup is of the same or-
der as the effect reported in the paper’s PPO-versus-GRPO comparison, which limits the
historical saturated comparison. The newer unsaturated comparison (§6.5.5) is baseline-


                                           224
specific and test-sensitive, with paired-t p = 0.016 but exact sign-flip p = 0.0625; neither
supports a general algorithm ranking.
Three limits remain. Lanes blocked on external access cannot be reproduced from this
repository, because the blocking resource is not in it. The source record identifies run-
ners for the six lanes completed on 27 September under outputs/finish_pending_2
026-09-27/. A rerun requires access to those sources, cloud accounts, a served copy of
the actor and the benchmark assets; the public document package alone does not supply
that execution environment. E6 also requires an available judge, and CORE-Bench’s
answer keys are deliberately kept out of the repository. The evidence-loss paths cannot
be reproduced in their original form, only re-implemented against surviving fixtures,
and that distinction should be preserved in any future citation. And figures originating
at the Semester 3 boundary describe the tree at that tag, so reproducing them requires
the worktree checkout rather than the current HEAD.



B.11       Primary-artefact recovery and availability

The 2 October checkout audit correctly recorded five named primary paths as absent
from that checkout. A bounded read-only search of the author’s Mac on 3 October
recovered all five originals in a separate live research checkout, plus an earlier pending-
experiments snapshot. The files were ignored by the local outputs/* rule. Their ab-
sence from the frozen cloud checkout and inspected Git/archive corpus was therefore a
scope boundary, not evidence of deletion. The frozen repository is unchanged; exact
recovered copies are retained privately and are not included in this public derivative.

export and the materialized copies. Source filesystem timestamps and existence come
from the Mac export; no historical Git commit or pre-execution attestation is invented.
These are recovered files, not newly generated experiment receipts.
Two source interpretation corrections follow. The E14 terminal note writes 2271/4428
= 0.5131043831902395, but that decimal equals 2,271/4,426. The unchanged thesis
keeps the official judged-row rate of 51.31% separate from the all-disposition rate of
51.29%; the original note is preserved with its inconsistency. The finish ledger explicitly
says the E4 rerun had not launched by its September 21 update, so it cannot substantiate
the later detailed zero-reward rerun account. That account retains its separate source
and raw-payload limitations in Appendix D.

covering 27 distinct file paths. For E8, 1,979 declared archive members match their
retained local copies, including 1,967 unique native-grade records; independent retal-

archive-member bindings match and the selected 97 distinct native traces each match
their receipt hash; the exported trace records independently sum to 88 utility passes and
zero handled errors. Historical partial traces are not pooled into that selection.

distinct dispositions, 4,426 accepted and 2,271 correct. The two excluded rows, 1195
and 2044, have recorded skip reasons and no Equivalence Judgement heading in the re-

and source hashes; it does not include all judge text or establish independent mathemat-

B.12      Fresh-Sampling Diagnostic: Frozen Contract and
          Audit

The new evidence in §7.4 is run V2 frozen-policy sampling study, a sampling-
only experiment with no weight updates. Its protocol, code, tests, expected cohort and
parser were hash-bound before allocation or generation at 18:15:03 UTC on 2 October
2026. The freeze covers 26 files. Prompt ordering was deterministic and independent
of scores, using seed 20261002; the V2 smoke and main cohorts are disjoint from each
other and from the failed V1 smoke, and the request-seed namespace is disjoint from
V1. The main cohort is 512 prompts and the independent smoke is 32 prompts, each
with eight initial plus 24 fresh draws.
Model and decoding. Model and tokenizer are Qwen/Qwen3-8B, both at revision b9
68826d9c46dd6066d109eabc6255188de91218. The dataset is openai/gsm8k, con-
figuration main, train split, revision 740312add88f781978c0658806c59bc2815b98
66 (7,473 rows). The model used BF16 with thinking disabled, temperature 0.7, top-p
0.8, top-k 20, min-p 0, a 1,024-generated-token cap and 4,096-token context. Each re-
quest had n = 1 and its own seed; batching grouped four prompts. The recorded stack


                                          227
used vLLM 0.11.0, PyTorch 2.8.0, Transformers 4.57.1, eager execution and disabled
prefix caching on one NVIDIA A100-SXM4-80GB. The supervised process completed
in approximately 26.6 minutes, including loading and both cohorts. Hardware/kernel
changes need not reproduce samples bit-for-bit.
Parser and review contract. The V2 parser accepts specified explicit final-answer
forms, normalises exact rational numbers and fails closed on malformed, conflicting
or invalid claims; it does not search arbitrary prose for a number. Gold parsing passed
offline on all 7,473 training rows before generation. This is a stricter contract than the
existing GSM8K test evaluator and must not be retrospectively substituted for it. Auto-
mated validation checked the frozen source and manifest bindings, cohort identity, com-
pleteness, ordered draw indices, unique seeds, reward recomputation and quality counts.
A separate set-based computation reproduced 461 eligible groups and 33 events. The
audit covered the first reward-changing response plus all eight initial responses for every
event. Two assistant reviewers handled disjoint event partitions; this does not mean that
each event received two independent reviews. The coordinator’s additional arithmetic
checks covered both recoveries and the ambiguous bread-rise case. The implementa-
tion field manual_review_complete records completion of this assistant review, not
human validation; residual model-review error is possible.
Descriptive checks, not controller validation. Among the original 461 homogeneous
groups, the combined-16 prefix became mixed for 15 (3.25%); the fresh-16-only com-
parison was mixed for 24 (5.21%). The all-32-parseable-and-uncapped subgroup was
12/439, selected on later outcomes and therefore not an unbiased replacement pri-
mary. Fixed Beta(1,1) and Beta(1/2,1/2) posterior-predictive assumptions assigned res-
cue probabilities of 72.73% and 49.41%, respectively, versus observed strict-reward
mixedness of 7.16%. Their descriptive Brier scores were 0.4964 and 0.2450, against
0.0716 for plug-in zero. These uncalibrated fixed-prior assumptions substantially over-
predict this endpoint; the exercise does not fit or validate a controller and does not vin-
dicate certainty of zero rescue. The post-hoc later-change check in the 20 parser-first
groups is exploratory and remains separate from the predeclared first-event numeric
subset.
Receipt locations and hash boundary.              Raw ordered completions are in
the run directory’s study.jsonl and smoke.jsonl; the per-prompt analysis is
analysis_final/per_prompt.csv. The final summary, validation and completed
audit preserve the unchanged raw primary endpoint alongside the conservative numeric
subset. The main study.jsonl has SHA-256 859ee12d9c146a3e9db65882b0a305d
123bd385c573fa6ac4692b25220ac422f. The summary and validation list the freeze,
manifest and source hashes, distinguishing the manifest’s canonical-content binding
from its file-byte checksum. Hashes bind the receipts to the trusted pre-execution freeze;


                                           228
they are not cryptographic attestation of GPU execution. Token counts and identities
were cross-checked, not independently decoded. The failed V1 smoke is retained as
engineering evidence and is never pooled with V2. These new sampling receipts are
separate from the original historical files subsequently recovered in §B.11.



B.13      C1 scientific source availability

The C1 analysis preserves the frozen 11/64 endpoint, the separate three conservative
numerical witnesses, all sixty-four case classifications and all eleven event records. The
included case ledgers and public scientific reference bindings retain disagreements and
hash-level scientific source descriptions. This public derivative excludes runtime alloca-
tion, session, deletion and raw-transfer records, account resource limits and operational
provenance. No live runtime or GPU execution is attested by publication of this docu-
ment.
The historical thesis and evidence archives remain unchanged outside this public pack-
age. The public derivative has its own editable Markdown, LaTeX, builder and selected
scientific ledgers. Appendix G is the complete scientific case index; Appendix I and the
public availability manifest explain omitted source categories.

chapter will misread a figure.

C.3        Four collisions worth flagging

The symbol 𝜀 carries three unrelated meanings. In the hyperparameter mapping it is
the PPO/GRPO clip range, default 0.2, swept over {0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.3, 0.5}. In the ZVF
definition it is a numerical tolerance, 10−6 , deciding whether a group’s sample variance
counts as degenerate. In the optimiser configuration it is the Adam epsilon, 10−8 . It also
appears additively in the advantage denominator, 𝜎𝑔 + 𝜀.
𝐾 is used two ways: in the formal treatment of ZVF it denotes the rollouts in a group,
a synonym for the group size elsewhere called 𝐺, whereas in the derivations appendix
𝐺 is the group size and 𝐾 is the number of successes — a design parameter in the one
case, an outcome in the other. The letter 𝑟 is similarly the reward, the LoRA rank (swept
over {4, 8, 16, 32, 64}) and the Pearson correlation.
Finally, ZVF is expanded two ways in the sources. The formal definition and the abstract
both read “Zero-Variance Fraction”, the expansion used here; one figure caption reads
“Zero-Value-Filtered”. The caption variant is noted only so that a reader meeting it is
not misled.

                                                 237
Appendix D

Campaign Lane Detail

This appendix holds the lane-by-lane record behind Chapter 8: provenance of the train-
ing run, per-lane serving and scoping notes, the engineering history behind individual
figures, the per-lane narratives of the six replacement scopes completed on 27 Septem-
ber 2026, the full E4 rerun audit, and the per-lane caveats of the small-scale reruns.
The descriptive figures agree with Chapter 8, and the two reporting rules of Chapter 8
(no pooling of original-contract and replacement-scope results, no unsupported preci-
sion) apply throughout. The E11 uncertainty calculation below is a new, reproducible
analysis of surviving paired verdicts. Six original files covering five previously miss-
ing basenames are now preserved in the withheld source archive (Appendix B §B.11).
Receipt-level fields and the supplementary stored-payload checks are distinguished be-
low from historical claims whose raw trajectories, training logs or supporting artifacts
remain unvalidated; recovery and count/hash checks are not new execution or regrading
of the experiments.



D.1      Actor identity, inference conditions and scoping
         notes

The design consequence of evaluating one actor for seed variance is specific: every suite
number reported in Chapter 8 comes from one actor, and where a lane sampled more
than one completion per task it did so to satisfy the suite’s own protocol, not to estimate
variance.
Inference conditions were reported per lane rather than imposed globally, and the lanes
differ widely. The recovered E10 native receipt declares a temperature of 0, a 32,768-
token context, 4,096 output tokens per request, 3,600 seconds of wall time. It also de-
clares max_generation_requests = 1,967, a ceiling on generation requests rather


                                           238
than a task count: the lane has 97 episodes. The earlier report separately gives 414
native requests (405 in the main run and 9 in the continuation) and says the collector
does not admit shorter native conditions. Those execution counts and collector claims
are not fields of the recovered receipt, and the cited terminal-readiness source remains
unrecovered. The E11 run was sampled at temperature 0.2, top-p 0.95, 1,024 maximum
output tokens and seed 1816, one sample per problem across all 312 prompts. The point
of recording them per lane is that the sampling regime is part of the result, not a detail
beneath it.
Two scoping notes complete the picture of what the actor has actually been measured
on. The E1 original-contract evaluation was a one-checkpoint evaluation whose 731
generations were produced by a mixed backend within a single run — 476 Tinker gen-
erations and 255 Modal vLLM generations — and that backend mix is recorded in the
original receipt. Separately, two small probes exist at other parameter settings but are
not suite results and are not reported as such: a base-model smoke run of the E11 har-
ness measured zero scored tasks, and a four-task pilot on the step-40 checkpoint (the last
training step, saved separately from stepfinal) returned four passes out of four with
zero extraction failures while carrying an unresolved outstanding blocker — the decon-
tamination receipt identifier for that pilot does not exist, and the split-manifest validator
still fails on it.



D.2      Training-run provenance

The earlier report attributed the adapter configuration to the local Weights & Biases di-
rectory for run withheld-id (withheld-training-log-directory), the run receipt
withheld actor-training receipt, and Hugging Face checkpoint branches. The
original outputs/public_portfolio_2026-09-05/training_identity_verif
ied.json is now recovered, with SHA-256 e340d5fe41b7b028dd78ea9f01f6da78
7c7a8cb968f5a0ac1b103a92b546ee86. It records run withheld-id, dataset revi-
sion bd1f0db6056091e99d5e28e3a7b3d05d77b21876ad65e5b31bf6f1b470e73e5
c, the two training-suite IDs and hashes for config.yaml, wandb-metadata.json and
output.log. It explicitly labels this TRAINING_CONFIG_VERIFIED_NOT_CONTENT_D
ECONTAMINATION and states that W&B captured the dirty checkout’s base commit but
did not upload the dirty source diff. The underlying W&B files and local run receipt
have not been recovered in this package, so a new byte-level match against those three
recorded hashes is not established here. The surviving E11 receipt verifies the evalu-
ated base/adapter identity, but does not reconstruct the missing training logs. Table 8.1
retains detailed training fields as earlier reported except where the recovered identity
record directly corroborates them.

                                            239
Four things remain unavailable or were never created, and they are listed so that no reader
fills them in:

    • The exact training code. The run records git commit 8fdade2f with uncommit-
      ted changes that were never saved. The pavlov_portfolio preset and its dataset
      builder do not exist at that commit; they first appear in commit 72b9336d3 on
      2026-08-14. The reward and data-pool code in Table 8.1 is therefore taken from
      the current tree, whose dataset hash does not match the recorded bd1f0db6…. It is
      the best reconstruction, not proof. The loss function and training loop are identical
      at 8fdade2f.
    • Which 80 training prompts were drawn. The draw used random.Random(809)
      over the 512-row pool, but the pool construction that ran is the unrecoverable code
      above.
    • The original sampler execution. The public package does not include execution-
      specific operational access paths and cannot replay that original route.
    • Any held-out training-time evaluation. None was run (evaluate_heldout
      false).

The run also departs from zvf-program/flagship/PAVLOV_EXPERIMENT_PROTOCO
L_2026-08-09.md. The protocol plans a learning-rate search and a twelve-suite cur-
riculum with 1,200/128 prompt and response tokens. This run used two suites, 40 steps,
1,536/384 tokens and a learning rate of 1 × 10−5 , and the protocol does not mention it;
only the budget matches.



D.3      Serving conditions: format limits and the scope of
         the stop-sequence mechanism

Table 8.2 reports substantial extraction and length failures; E11’s aggregate extraction
count is verifiable in the surviving receipt and E8’s aggregate diagnostics are now cor-
roborated by its recovered original. The supplementary validation independently cor-
roborates E8’s stored grade and finish-reason counts, but the 1,259 parse-none count re-
mains diagnostic-only. These checks do not validate every serving field, response body
or cited finish-era source. The reported absence of role-token run-on in E1’s single-turn
Tinker generations does not establish a necessary condition for the mechanism. The E4
bridge code and recorded examples support a concrete failure mode, while the corrected
shim’s nonzero outcome comes from one task against 100 bridge trials. Task selection,
serving path and caps differ, so the fraction of the original aggregate zero caused by
the defect remains unidentified. For other bridge-served lanes exposure is possible, not

                                           240
demonstrated. This matches the scope qualification in outputs/e1_e14_small_scal
e_2026-09-26/E4/BRIDGE_STOP_TOKEN_FINDING.md.
Three non-bridge failure attributions are corrected (Table E.1). E11’s 150 extraction
failures occur under a 1,024-token cap with thinking on, a plausible contributor whose
effect is not isolated; E14’s capped responses are correct at 34.5% against 85.9% for un-
capped ones; and 593 of E1’s 713 submitted patches are corrupt for git apply. Lane
detail is in §D.4 and §D.5. The runners now record per-sample finish reasons and trun-
cation (E11, E14) and repair and record patch-apply status (E1), but no stored score was
regenerated.



D.4      Strictly complete scopes: lane detail

The ledger lists E11 both among its “replacement scopes” and among its original-
contract results; Table 8.A lists it as original-public-subset. It is one run and one result,
not two.
E8 — LAB-Bench, public split. The recovered outputs/public_portfolio_20
26-09-05/labbench_final_diagnostics.json (SHA-256 7d7d0bd4716253421
2f7526db548e1f5d42bbe16554bc2e29115f0d9cf5af941) directly records the fol-
lowing per-category accuracy, correct counts and coverage: TableQA 0.7869 (192/244);
FigQA 0.5138 (93/181); ProtocolQA 0.2500 (27/108); SuppQA 0.1829 (15/82); LitQA2
0.1759 (35/199); DbQA 0.1308 (68/520); SeqQA 0.0333 (20/600); and CloningScenar-
ios 0 (0/33). No category is incomplete. Coverage per category is at or above 0.9878
everywhere, and the CloningScenarios coverage is exactly 1.0 with zero correct. The
receipt also records zero parser errors, 1,960 responses marked “sure” against 7 marked
“unsure”, and a completion profile of 1,086 length-truncated finishes against 881 stop
finishes. The diagnostic’s correct and incorrect fields partition all 1,967 rows, so the
headline is an end-to-end harness score over the full split. The earlier account treats the
1,259 unparsed responses as incorrect and supplies the conditional 450/708 sensitivity;
that response-level membership has not been independently regraded. The diagnostic
binds a native-receipt hash (b08995d5…) and a graded-responses hash (8a61def9…).
The supplementary Mac validation locates the native receipt at the declared hash and
checks 1,979 declared archive members against both archive and local bytes. Indepen-
dently tallying its 1,967 exported grade records reproduces 450 correct, 1,517 incorrect,
all category denominators and correct counts, 1,960 sure, 7 unsure, 1,086 length finishes,
881 stop finishes and zero parser errors. Those exported records have no parsed-answer
field, so they do not independently establish the 1,259 parse-none count or its response-
level membership. No native scorer was rerun.

E10 — AgentDojo, benign utility scope. The recovered native receipt (SHA-256 a40c
90015200a389ce0767b14a78f9409330a0ae8615cb4822ef9b54656ebaa0) records
missing_evaluation_ids empty and native_handled_error_count zero. It car-
ries run-manifest hash 00e4228f4b757dc32b69b74f05a88348151f13185b470c5f
00e31fdaa830ef30 and fixes the metric as benign_task_utility, benchmark ver-
sion v1.2.2, attack and defence null, security_evaluation false and heldout_claim
false. The earlier statement that all three protocol fields were null is corrected (Table
E.1). There is no upstream benchmark commit field in the recovered receipt, so the
earlier attribution of commit 089ed468cf3ed0322acc66b0211f26d9d90dbf60 to it is
unsupported. The episode decomposition, banking 16, slack 21, travel 20 and workspace
40, is absent from the native receipt but is independently corroborated by the supple-
mentary export’s 97 selected trace records. Their utility/error fields tally to 88 passes
and zero handled errors. All 97 evaluation IDs map uniquely to exact-hash archived
traces, selecting 94 from the original collection and 3 from the explicit continuation;
extra historical partial traces are not pooled. The Mac validation also reports all 525
archive-member pins matching. These are stored-trace checks, not re-execution of the
native evaluator or independent semantic grading. Its decontamination block records T
RAINING_INVENTORY_ABSENT; empty match lists without the training inventory do not
establish absence of overlap.
The following collector history is retained from the earlier report. The supplementary
validation locates the continuation terminal-evidence file on the Mac and matches its de-
clared hash, and the exported 94-plus-3 trace mapping supports the completed-episode
accounting. It does not reproduce the collector’s 18 offline tests or export the full snap-
shot, readiness and continuation-result contents, so the detailed repair and test claims
below remain historical. These sources are still absent from the frozen repository. An
earlier attempt reportedly terminated with 94 of 97 episodes and score null, missing
workspace tasks 37 through 39. The cause was a confirmed collector fault rather than a
model failure: verified task receipts recorded absolute /cache/... trace paths, and the
frozen summary recomputed those paths on the local host, so every completed receipt
failed comparison after extraction. The repair was deliberately minimal — the original
archive and every extracted receipt and trace byte are unchanged, and a narrowly scoped
adapter rewrites only the freshly computed task_result.native_trace_path to the
verified canonical remote path before the frozen summary compares it, leaving native
task code, environment, prompt, result parsing, utility values and handled-error policy
untouched. The revised collector passed 18 offline tests, independently re-run in 0.507
seconds, and the continuation run that closed episodes 37 through 39 was executed in
the reported continuation with the native wrapper hash 11a8b61631390d9b20decd42
63008e5a4c6f648a033d314384bac9e5d2456d77.


                                           242
E11 — VerilogEval. The upstream NVlabs/verilog-eval harness ran at revision c498
220d0a52248f8e3fdffe279075215bde2da6, using Icarus Verilog 12.0 at revision
4fd5291632232fbe1ba49b2c26bb6b2bf1c6c9cf and Verilator 5.050. Pass ver-
dicts were determined deterministically: for each pinned problem the single sample01
sv-iv-test log was read and marked PASS only when it contained a full-line “Mis-
matches: 0 in samples” verdict, with missing or non-matching logs marked FAIL. The
upstream sv-iv-analyze summariser could not be used because it fails on a missing
optional langchain dependency; that failure is recorded in the receipt and does not affect
the aggregation, which reads the simulator logs directly. An extraction failure writes an
empty candidate file that always fails.
E11 design-cluster uncertainty. The verifier.receipts.code-complete-iccad
2023.log_receipts and verifier.receipts.spec-to-rtl.log_receipts map-
pings in the surviving full receipt contain exactly the same 156 design IDs. Matching by
ID gives 54 designs passing both framings, 81 failing both, 13 passing code-completion
only and 8 passing specification-to-RTL only. The 312 prompt outcomes are therefore
not 312 independent design observations. The descriptive pass fraction is unchanged
at (2 × 54 + 13 + 8)/312 = 129/312. For uncertainty over design composition, resam-
ple 156 whole design pairs uniformly with replacement per replicate, retain both ob-
served outcomes, and calculate their summed passes divided by 312. With 200,000
replicates from Python random.Random(20261002), the 2.5th and 97.5th percentiles
are 0.3429487 and 0.4871795, reported as [0.343, 0.487]. Quantiles use linear inter-
polation at index (B − 1)p. The standard-library script platform_hybrid/experim
ents/scripts/e11_design_cluster_bootstrap.py reproduces the calculation;
the adjacent e11_design_cluster_bootstrap.json preserves method, seed, receipt
SHA-256, pair counts and all 156 paired verdicts, and the script falls back to it when
the ignored receipt is absent. Run it from the repository root with python platform_
hybrid/experiments/scripts/e11_design_cluster_bootstrap.py. The exact
upper bound depends on drawing with random.choices, because the 97.5th percentile
falls on the boundary between 151/312 and 152/312; drawing with randrange under
the same seed gives 0.4839744. The script previously cited under outputs/PES_Phas
e2_Third_Review_2026-09-24/analysis/ was never committed.
This interval assumes exchangeable underlying designs and conditions on one check-
point, one generation per framing and the observed protocol. It does not estimate varia-
tion across training seeds, decoding seeds or protocols, and does not establish general-
ization beyond comparable designs. Conditional on the complete fixed benchmark and
these recorded outputs, 129/312 is simply the observed fraction. The parseable-only
129/162 remains descriptive: the receipt records 150 extraction failures only in aggre-
gate, not per-design extraction indicators, so it cannot support a cluster-aware interval


                                           243
for that conditional rate.
E11 failure attribution. The 150 extraction failures are not primarily answer-format
failures (corrected; see Table E.1). run_e11_full in zvf-program/flagship/mo
dal_e1_e14.py used max_tokens = 1,024 and did not pass enable_thinking, so
the model’s template default, thinking on, applied. No per-sample finish reason was
recorded, but the receipt’s 312,622 response tokens over 312 samples give a mean of
1,002 of 1,024, so nearly every generation reached the cap. On the 50 items of the
26 September small-scale rerun, the receipt’s verdicts give 23/50 and the same trained
adapter, served by vLLM with thinking off, temperature 0 and a 4,096-token cap, scores
33/50 with no extraction failures and every response ending on a stop; the discordant
pairs are 11 against 1 (exact McNemar p ≈ 0.006). Engine and temperature changed
along with the budget, and 50 of 312 prompts is a subset, so 129/312 stays the canonical
score and no budget-corrected score is reported. The extractor also stripped reasoning
only when a closing </think> was present; for a response truncated inside its reasoning
it graded any module … endmodule found in the draft, and only response hashes were
kept, so it cannot be determined how many of the 162 extracted candidates or the 129
passes came from reasoning drafts. The runner now refuses extraction from an untermi-
nated reasoning block, makes the thinking mode explicit and records response text, finish
reason and truncation per sample; the stored result was produced before those changes.
The earlier unclustered Wilson intervals on pooled and parseable-only outcomes are not
used.
The E11 lane also carries the campaign’s clearest example of a scoring decision taken
for evidential rather than numerical reasons. The all-problems reference sweep — run-
ning the shipped reference implementation of every problem through both simulators
— returned 311 of 312. One problem, verilog_eval/spec-to-rtl/Prob099_m201
4_q6c, fails to elaborate against its own test bench under both simulators, with Icarus
reporting that ports Y2 and Y4 are not ports of good1; no candidate implementation can
pass that task, and the upstream defect biases pass@k downwards by at most 0.64 per-
centage points on the specification-to-RTL split (1/156) and by 0.13 percentage points
overall (129/311 − 129/312). Excluding it yields 129/311 = 0.4148. That corrected fig-
ure is not canonical. The recorded amendment of 2026-08-22 states the reason plainly:
no immutable benchmark-level justification was captured for the exclusion, so the raw
129/312 result is canonical and the 129/311 value is retained only as a noncanonical
sensitivity; no samples and no verifier artifacts were changed by the amendment. The
ledger’s integrity check confirms that the component split sums correctly to the canoni-
cal total.
E14 — Omni-MATH replacement scope. The surviving consolidated ledger and ou
tputs/verification/LEDGER_CODE_CHECK_2026-09-19.json record 4,426/4,428


                                          244
accepted rows and accuracy 0.5131043831902395, consistent with 2,271 passes among
4,426 judged rows. Counting the remaining two as failures gives 2,271/4,428 = 51.29 per
cent. The recovered terminal note (SHA-256 14b8308df159f9c86a3a58d724b8152a
5f44f098c57a824637fcbe5ae6f3602a) identifies omni-01195-a6cbdc4ee3c8694b
and omni-02044-612ddacaa39d91fe as the two unaccepted rows and records that
Omni-Judge outputs were truncated before an Equivalence Judgement section. It states
that both had recorded skip reasons, that omission followed the native scorer, that no re-
judge was performed and that the lane closed with all 4,428 dispositions. This now sup-
ports the terminal decision and explains the earlier parser-failure terminology at receipt
level. The supplementary Mac-side validation now inspects the stored rows and exports
the two exclusion records, each lacking the Equivalence Judgement heading and carry-
ing the recorded omission reason; no mathematical answer was independently rejudged.
The note’s score_impact nevertheless writes 2271/4428 = 0.5131043831902395;
the decimal is instead 2,271/4,426. The recovered finish ledger repeats the inconsistent
pairing of 2,271/4,428 with 51.31%. Both sources remain unchanged, and the thesis
preserves the corrected distinction between 51.29% over all dispositions and 51.31%
over judged rows (Table E.1). The supplementary export identifies the disposition and
judged-row payloads and the scoring-recheck file on the Mac, with hashes. Its traversal
records 4,428 valid rows in each of the disposition, judged-row and new-judge-response
JSONL files, with 4,426 accepted, 2,271 correct and two skipped dispositions. The
recorded native-score fields explicitly distinguish denominator 4,426 from the all-4,428
diagnostic. The full E14 rows are not exported here; the cloud check can independently
verify the exported arithmetic and two exclusion records, but cannot independently re-
count all 4,428 rows from this export.
E14 generation budget. The actor cap was 2,048 tokens (zvf-program/flagship/p
ublic_omni_math_native.py), and 2,982 of 4,428 responses reached it. Recounting
native_dispositions.jsonl, capped responses are correct in 1,029 of 2,982 (34.5%)
and uncapped ones in 1,242 of 1,446 (85.9%) (corrected reading; see Table E.1). Diffi-
culty confounds the split, because harder problems both run longer and fail more often,
so it does not estimate what a larger budget would score. It does mean the 51.29% head-
line is substantially a property of the budget and not a budget-free capability estimate.
The judge’s 300-token cap is what truncated the two unjudged reports. The runner did
not record capped status; it now writes a per-task cap_status.json and an accuracy
_by_actor_cap receipt field, without altering the stored grades.




                                           245
D.5      Original-contract results: lane detail

E1 — SWE-bench Pro. The denominator is the complete 731-row test split of
ScaleAI/SWE-bench_Pro at revision 7ab5114912baf22bb098818e604c02fe7ad2
c11f. Within that denominator the run produced 713 native evaluations, fourteen gen-
eration failures and four lost generation artifacts; those eighteen non-native outcomes are
retained in the denominator rather than trimmed, so the figure is a rate over all 731 tasks
and not over the subset that graded cleanly. Those 713 native evaluations are mostly not
evaluations of an applied fix (corrected reading; see Table E.1). The prompt asked for
unified diffs with concrete line ranges but showed source without line numbers and with
elided regions, and the runner’s diff validator checked syntax but not that hunk-header
line counts match the hunk bodies. Parsing the stored candidates.json with git
apply --stat rejects 593 of 713 patches as corrupt (“corrupt patch at line …”), and
133 carry a placeholder index 1234567..abcdefg line. The official entry script runs
a plain git apply -v without --recount and without stopping on failure, so for those
593 instances the tests ran against the unpatched base. With --recount, 711 of 713
parse. Both resolved instances are among the 120 that parse as submitted. Apply status
was not captured by the run, so whether every parseable patch applied to its repository is
not known. The 2/731 is retained as the measured score, but it mostly reflects diff-header
arithmetic rather than issue resolution. The runner now recounts hunk headers before
submission, rejects count mismatches and records parse status for the raw and submit-
ted patch; the stored run predates that repair. The declared licence for the dataset is null
in the lane’s own receipt. The evaluation harness is scaleapi/SWE-bench_Pro-os at
commit ca10a60a5fcae51e6948ffe1485d4153d421e6c5, and the 731-task scoring
run used the 731 official run scripts unmodified on native linux/amd64 Modal workers.
A 1,200-second timeout, against the official 120, was used only in an earlier single-task
harness check that ran the gold patch under arm64 QEMU emulation. That check is
recorded as not a model score, so the timeout deviation does not apply to the 2/731
figure.
E2 — FrontierSWE. The scientific result remains partial at 1/17 tasks. The public pack-
age does not supply the original execution route or privately held operational records.
E4 — BankerToolBench. The surviving historical summaries and recovered finish
ledger report restored public artifacts and 1,175 manifest checks passing. The recov-
ered ledger specifically reports 0/6 pass3/pass16 archive artifacts available, with only
TINKER_BRIDGE.json verifying. This supports the historical archive-loss account, not
a fresh verification of the archive or a replacement of the receipt-only 0.3115 metric.
E7 — BinaryAudit. The recovered finish ledger preserves both the terminal table’s
“1/46 attempted” and its superseded historical snapshot’s “1/28 errored”. The termi-


                                            246
nal figure is retained in Chapter 8. Recovery makes both source statements directly
inspectable, but the ledger does not explain their denominator difference and the raw
attempt inventory has not been revalidated here.
E9 — MLE-bench. Coverage without complete grading is not a score, and the lane
records it as such. Behind the coverage number sit 97 legacy receipts across 50 compe-
tition identifiers, of which 41 graded receipts cover 40 unique competitions; the ledger’s
integrity check verifies precisely that decomposition. The separate merged-vLLM arm
holds seven receipts, of which exactly one is a valid grade (H&M, 0.02132) and six are
invalid submissions, and the integrity check confirms that arm is recorded separately
with one graded row. One old Spooky Author submission is absent locally although the
later run’s complete submission is retained.



D.6      Replacement-scope lane narratives, 27 September
         2026

Three things changed in how the lanes were executed, and each is recorded as a deviation
in the lane’s own result file. First, where a fail-closed controller blocked only on paper-
work that the directive had superseded, the lane used a thin runner that calls the bench-
mark’s native loop and native grader directly. Second, compute moved off AWS: E2, E6
and E9 ran on Google Cloud virtual machines, E1’s grading ran in Modal sandboxes, and
E13 ran locally. Third, every lane was served the same actor, campaign-actor-bf16
(the base model with the seed809 adapter merged, §8.1), through vLLM with the non-
thinking chat template. No lane went through the Tinker bridge implicated in the E4
failure mechanism (§8.1.2, §8.5), so that defect is not expected here, although the lanes’
result files do not record their stop settings. The shared endpoint served at a 32,768-
token context. E5 used a dedicated 65,536-token copy of the same weights because its
banking prompts exceed 32k.
E1 — SWE-bench Multilingual. This run covers the 16 wave-10 recovery tasks and
the 174 tasks that the 12 September waves never reached, 190 tasks in all. The actor
resolved one (phpoffice__phpspreadsheet-3463), which is 1/190 = 0.53 per cent.
Only 57 of the 190 produced a patch that applied and could be graded: 120 failed at patch
application or evaluation and 13 produced an empty patch. Over graded tasks alone the
rate is 1/57 = 1.75 per cent ([0.3, 9.3]). Thirteen tasks produced no generation and were
scored as failures: eight exceeded the 32,768-token serving context and five hit provider
errors. The earlier waves covered the other 110 tasks, with 4 resolved (3.64 per cent,
[1.42, 8.98]). They used the same task universe, adapter and grader family, but served
at a 65,536-token context, and their source-context collector was lost and had to be re-


                                           247
implemented for this run. Because the contract differs, the two runs are not combined,
and the lane does not report a 300-task figure.
E2 — CORE-Bench, hard diﬀiculty. The actor answered every question correctly on
27 of the 45 codeocean_hard capsules, a task accuracy of 0.600. At the question level
it answered 53 of 79 correctly: 28 of 33 written questions and 25 of 46 vision questions.
Thirty-two tasks ended with the agent calling finish, ten at the 150-turn cap and three
at the 8,100-second time limit. The agent was a thin tool loop (bash, query_image,
finish) rather than the benchmark’s reference CORE-Agent scaffold, and the same
actor answered the vision queries. Thirty tasks never reached a machine because of a
local DNS outage, and a driver crash then interrupted ten of their reruns. All thirty were
rerun from scratch, and every aborted attempt is archived. Because a leak of the answers
would inflate this figure, the lane checked for one. No agent command referenced any
host that serves the answers. Each capsule’s results/ directory is stripped before the
agent starts, and the answer keys are held off the agent’s machine. Two qualifications
remain. Twelve of the 27 correct tasks left no files in the capsule’s results directory,
which the native grader does not require. Some vision questions are categorical and
could be guessed. With n = 45 the interval spans 27 percentage points.
E5 — Tau3. Eight of 97 tasks succeeded, a pass^1 of 0.082. Twenty-five tasks ended
in infrastructure errors: fourteen exceeded the 65,536-token context and eleven returned
an empty assistant message. Their cause is not established, and they count as failures.
An accidental resume of the run overwrote 24 of those error records in the results file;
their identifiers and causes were rebuilt from the run log. Over the 72 tasks without
such an error the native pass^1 is 8/72 = 0.111, reported as a sensitivity figure only.
The 20 tasks cleaned before 19 September were rerun under the same protocol rather
than carried over, and scored 1/20. The user simulator was gpt-4.1-2025-04-14 at
temperature 0.
E6 — WebArena. All 812 tasks were run, and 90 succeeded, giving 0.111. The fig-
ure is a lower bound. Of the 114 tasks whose native evaluator calls an LLM judge,
108 could not be graded, because the judge was unavailable for the run; the other six
were resolved to zero by a non-judge check. Forty-one tasks failed with a harness-level
browser timeout. Both groups count as failures. The graded-only rate is 90/704 = 0.128
([0.105, 0.155]). If every ungraded task had passed, the rate would be 198/812 = 0.244.
With the 41 harness errors counted as failures, the true figure lies between 0.111 and
0.244, and nearer the lower end if the ungraded tasks succeed at the graded rate. The
native judge model (gpt-4-1106-preview) is retired, so a later regrade would substi-
tute a different judge. Most failures are early stops by the native loop: the same action
three times (237), the same typing three times (103), the step limit (101), and three con-
secutive parse failures (98). The agent was limited to 384 output tokens per step, the


                                           248
few-shot examples were passed as user and assistant turns because the chat template re-
jects mid-conversation system messages, and sites were not reset between tasks, as in
the native runner. The last 103 shopping-admin tasks were split across three containers
of the same official site image to finish in time.
E9 — ML-Dev-Bench. Ten of the 34 released tasks passed their native validators, 0.294.
The agent was the benchmark’s OpenHands CodeActAgent, limited to 50 iterations. No
task errored or timed out, but only after one harness repair: OpenHands 0.21.0 hangs
when it truncates its context window, and a one-line upstream fix was backported without
touching prompts, validators or tasks. Thirty-nine of 1,045 model calls exceeded the
32,768-token context and were absorbed by that truncation path. A relay between the
agent and the actor forced the non-thinking template and restored tool-call text that the
server’s parser had dropped. An earlier four-task batch that hung before the repair is
excluded.
E13 — BALROG. All 255 native episodes were played and graded, and the BALROG
score is 26.1 per cent progression (standard error 1.77; a stratified bootstrap gives [22.7,
29.5]). BALROG’s score is the mean of six per-environment means, not a mean over
episodes. By environment the actor reached BabyAI 82.0 per cent (n = 50), Baba Is
AI 25.0 (n = 120), Crafter 22.7 (n = 10), TextWorld 16.9 (n = 30), MiniHack 10.0 (n =
40) and NetHack 0.0 (n = 5). Sampling followed the recorded protocol at temperature
1.0, but with thinking off where the original run had it on. A 2,000-step cap was placed
on NetHack for cost; all five NetHack episodes ended on their own before it, so the
cap never took effect. Episodes stopped by an operational stop or a hung worker were
restarted from scratch with fresh seeds. The result file records 262 episode attempt
records for the 255 scored episodes, of which 7 are marked killed for restart (n_killed
_for_restart_records); its caveat text instead counts 11 restarts (one NetHack and
five Baba Is AI episodes at the 01:40 UTC stop, plus five hung workers), and the file
does not reconcile the two counts. If hangs correlate with episode difficulty, that is a
mild selection risk. The run used macOS arm64 builds of the pinned sources rather than
the Linux image. The 26.1 figure is on a different scope from the 26-episode small-scale
E13 rows of Tables 8.A and 8.B and is not compared with them.
The launch-pending and quota labels of 19 September no longer describe any lane.



D.7      E4 rerun: reported audit and causal limits

The recovered finish ledger records E4 as CLOSED_PARTIAL, preserving the 19 Septem-
ber close-out. It reports the pass3/pass16 archive as missing (0/6 archive artifacts; only
TINKER_BRIDGE.json verifies), and keeps the 0.3115 recovery metric as receipt-only


                                            249
evidence. Its updates through 21 September state that the full 100-trial rerun had not
launched. That snapshot cannot substantiate the later completed rerun. The surviving
26 September E4 result’s prior_base_run field instead reports a 21 September run
with 100 trials, one error and mean reward 0.0. Its source job receipt remains unrecov-
ered in this package, so the detailed account below remains historical rather than a fresh
trajectory-level verification.
The earlier report describes the rerun as the base model with a zero-initialised LoRA
snapshot, equivalent to base sampling with no training steps, through the Modal
bridge /v1/responses path at --n-concurrent 2 It reports 100 of 100 trials, one
NonZeroAgentExitCodeError, a mean reward of exactly 0.0, all one hundred trials in
the 0.0 bucket and a runtime of 3 hours 29 minutes. The surviving outputs/e1_e14
_small_scale_2026-09-26/E4/result.json (prior_base_run) corroborates the
100 trials, one error, zero mean and reward buckets, and identifies the serving path. The
exact launch configuration, named error type, cap and runtime are not established by
that secondary field and cannot be rechecked against the unrecovered outputs/e4_ba
nker_toolbench/official_repo_ff6db552/jobs/btb-banking-tasks-tinker
-bridge/result.json. None of those later-run details is attributed to the recovered
pending-state snapshot.
The historical audit reports a failure mechanism across the one hundred trajectories.
Agents reportedly terminate after 2 to 14 steps, with a median of about 6, and produce no
deliverables/ directory; the verifier consequently short-circuits to 0.0 on the message
“No deliverables found”. Twenty-seven or more of the trajectories end in role-token
degeneration of the form user user assistant ... repetition. The record at the
time read this as the base model being unable to sustain the opencode tool-use loop. A
later bridge check exposes an alternative explanation, so that reading is not identified
by this uncontrolled run. The bridge’s /v1/responses path passed no stop sequences
and decoded with special tokens removed, so generation ran past the end-of-turn token
into invented user, tool_response and assistant turns, which is exactly the role-
token degeneration observed. The corrected shim scored 0.4087 on btb-19b3361c,
but that single-task success is compared with 100 bridge trials under different tasks,
serving path and caps. The contrast supports a plausible mechanism that can cause
zero-reward trajectories; it does not quantify the defect’s contribution to the full zero or
rule out model limitations. The reported no-deliverables check is consistent with fail-
closed grading, but the absent raw trajectories prevent a fresh verification of the cause
of every zero. No unconfounded full-suite BankerToolBench capability estimate exists
for this actor family; the other measurement is the six-task subset of Tables 8.A and 8.B.
The same bridge served E2, E5, E7 and the E9 streaming arm, so Table 8.2 marks those
lanes as exposed to the same defect. In particular, the rerun’s 0.0 must not be set against


                                            250
the original single-task outcome as though the two were a controlled pairing: they are
different configurations under different runtime conditions, and the campaign makes no
comparison between them.
The historical rerun report records 16.35 million prompt tokens and 2.18 million com-
pletion tokens. These token details cannot be rechecked against the absent job receipt.
The surviving outputs/e1_e14_small_scale_2026-09-26/E4/result.json (pri
or_base_run) repeats the 100 trials, one error and zero mean; its verifiable_from
_raw flag is a historical assertion and does not establish raw-artifact availability in this
repository.



D.8      Externally blocked lanes and verification limits

Seven original contracts remain externally blocked: SDAB, BinaryAudit, LifeSciBench,
AgentHarm, AppBench, OpenReward and FrontierMath. These are coverage limita-
tions, not scored failures or evidence about model ability. Replacement scopes retain
their separate names and denominators. Operational access correspondence, contact
destinations, account capacity, request identifiers and account costs are omitted from
this public derivative.
The historical record reports eleven deterministic checks covering selected arithmetic,
coverage and source bindings. This is not an independent re-execution of the bench-
marks, and it does not establish universal receipt availability. The original campaign
recovery and stored-payload checks have the bounded scope stated in Appendix B.



D.9      Small-scale reruns: detail and per-lane caveats

The small-scale protocol records retention of each lane’s items, raw outputs and grader
logs for recomputation. This is a source-corpus retention claim, not a statement that all
payloads accompany this public package. Its included-file boundary is recorded in the
public availability manifest (Appendix I).
For E12 the 151 rubric items come from only six generated applications, so they are not
independent. The cluster-level test is an exact sign-flip test over the six per-app pass-rate
differences (−0.042, +0.030, 0.000, −0.160, 0.000, −0.042), and it gives p = 0.25. The
E12 grader is an LLM judge that is the same Qwen/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B base model as
the base arm, so self-preference could favour the base arm. Two judge versions disagree
widely (0.536 against 0.808 on the Tinker base run; E12/result.json). The E12 rates
and difference are therefore judge-dependent, and no independent judge was run. For E4,


                                            251
all six tasks score 0 in both arms except btb-19b3361c; a Wilson interval is not valid
for a mean of fractional [0, 1] rewards, and a bootstrap over one nonzero difference in
six is nondegenerate but has little empirical support: the stored percentile interval is
[−0.0799, 0.0], with its shape determined by resampling that one task. The main table
therefore omits an inferential interval. For E13, in 22 of the 26 episodes the progression
is identical in the two arms. No lane-level difference is interpreted as evidence that
the adapter improves or degrades performance unless its test excludes zero, and none is
extrapolated to the full suite.
The principal caveat for each lane, as recorded in its result file, is listed below; the full
list is in each lane’s result.json.

    • E1. New base-model arm; not comparable to or poolable with the original 2/731.
      Original inference temperature was 0.2; this public subset cannot replay its private
      execution route.
    • E2. Substitute benchmark; never pool with or present as the FrontierSWE lane
      score (original: 1/17 tasks, replay 0.8628). Original execution access is outside
      this public package.
    • E3. New base-model arm; never pool with or present as the original
      SDAB/seed809 lane score.
    • E4. Small n=6 of 100; one nonzero task, so no interval is reported (the result file’s
      Wilson95 on summed fractional reward is not valid for a [0,1] mean).
    • E5. n=10, not the requested 20: the E4+E5 6M Tinker-token cap was exhausted
      (E4 1.56M, abandoned APEX attempt 2.33M, tau2 2.05M).
    • E6. Tasks drawn by random.Random(20260926).sample from all 128 sorted
      miniwob env ids; episode seeds 0-2.
    • E7. New base-model arm; never pool with or present as the original lane score.
    • E8. New base-model arm; not comparable to or poolable with the seed809-
      adapter 1967/1967 run (22.88%); the public subset cannot replay the original ex-
      ecution route.
    • E9. Competition pool: 6 low-split competitions with small non-image-heavy data
      (detecting-insults, jigsaw-toxic, leaf-classification, nomad2018, random-acts-of-
      pizza, spooky); 5 drawn by random.Random(20260926).sample. n=5 so the CI is
      very wide.
    • E10. New base-model arm on AgentHarm itself; the seed809-adapter lane figure
      (AgentDojo benign utility 88/97 = 90.72%; original execution route not supplied
      publicly) is a different benchmark and not comparable.
    • E11. New base-model arm; not comparable to the retained adapter receipt
      129/312 = 41.35% (that run used thinking-default template, temperature 0.2, all
      312).

                                            252
• E12. Public tasks, not held-out. Label mandatory. New base-model arm; never
  pool with the original lane.
• E13. New base-model arm; not comparable to or pooled with the original
  seed809-LoRA E13 plan (21-episode BabyAI admission set).
• E14. New base-model arm; the prior 2271/4428 = 51.29% figure (51.31% on the
  official scorer’s 4426 judged rows) is not comparable (different actor, full split).

Appendix E

Corrections to Internal Source
Documents

The numbers in this thesis are drawn from the project’s internal source documents: the
unpublished P1–P8 reports and their section files, audit summaries and result files. Some
of those documents contain errors — a wrong sign, a test applied to the wrong unit of
analysis, an interval that does not describe its own estimate, or a label that does not match
the rows it covers. The chapters report only the corrected value and mark it “(corrected;
see Table E.1)”. This appendix records, for each correction, what the source states,
what the thesis uses instead, and the basis for the change, so that a reader who opens
the source can reconcile it with the thesis. Caveats about the evidence itself, as opposed
to errors in a source document, stay in the chapters. Paths are relative to the repository
root; “sections/” is platform_hybrid/paper/sections/ and “results/” is platform
_hybrid/experiments/results/.
